    Chapter 1

    Chapter Notes See the end of the chapter for notes Oxford University, Oxfordshire.

    September 2023.

    Agatha hadn’t chosen to be smart, to ace her A Levels and get into Oxford. English literature, history, psychology. They were A Levels she considered easy; she didn’t actually want to do anything with them. Three fucking A*’s. Her downfall. Her unbelievably poetic demise. It was not up for debate that Agatha would be off to Oxford. It was done; Evanora had her bags packed before the term even began. Why didn’t she argue? Present her case? Tell her mother she couldn’t go to Oxford to read for fucking history because she didn’t give a fuck about it.

    Agatha was sick of small towns where everybody knew her name and her family, was sick of rubbing shoulders with the filthy fucking rich. “Those are your people,” her mother said. “The only way you will escape them is if you move abroad.”

    “Then I shall move abroad.”

    “Over my dead body,” Evanora grit out. “Who will look after the estate when I’m on work trips, Aggie?”

    Agatha hated that goddamn nickname.

    “Oh, I don’t know. Perhaps your husband?” Agatha snarked. “Do not bring him up,” Evanora snarled. “You’re going to Oxford and that’s that.”

    Agatha had enough generational wealth to build an entirely different life wherever she saw fit. She couldn’t understand why she hadn’t done that. Instead of moving into her stuffy Christ Church dorm room, she could’ve been moving into a beach villa in Bora Bora. The reason why she was here must’ve been bigger than her. At least, that’s what Agatha told herself. God, she was so bored. How could she be bored? She’d only been at Oxford a month, there were still two years and eleven months left. How long could she keep up the pretences with the same people she’d grown up with? She hated every single one of them. It didn’t matter that Agatha sat with them in the dining halls, partied with them, went to class with them; nothing could make the experience pleasant. One time at a gathering she’d gotten so drunk she was certain that hatred would slip beneath the thick layer of substances, it did not.

    Perhaps it never would.

    Just the same fucking bullshit-

    “Are you alright?”

    Agatha furrowed her eyebrows. That accent, Spanish, in a voice she didn’t recognise. Agatha looked up to see the most beautiful pools of amber. “You’ve been sat here for a while,” the girl went on.

    Had she?

    The midnight blue sky should’ve given her a hint. When had the evening morphed into night? Agatha tilted her head up at the humongous Radcliffe Camera she sat beneath and her head spun. Maybe she should’ve laid off the wine at dinner.

    “Fine,” Agatha muttered.

    The brunette’s lips lifted into a smirk. “Not sure I believe you.” Without invitation, she sat down beside Agatha on the neatly trimmed grass, a stack of books she’d probably just obtained from the library in her lap. Agatha scanned the title of the first textbook: biochemistry. So the stranger was smart and pretty.

    “Why are you out here by yourself? Where are your friends?” Agatha would’ve liked to say she didn’t have friends but held her tongue. “You sure do ask a lot of questions,” Agatha griped.

    Her companion scoffed with an eyeroll. “You people are all the same.” Agatha’s head jerked at that. “What the bloody hell does that mean?” “That you can’t get your fucking heads out your arses,” the girl supplied easily. “I asked if you’re okay and you’re talking to me like I’m the shit on the bottom of your shoe.”

    “I’m so sorry,” Agatha replied mockingly. “I should’ve been nicer, you look like someone who needs coddling.”

    The brunette scoffed again, picking up her books and getting to her feet. “Try not to die out here.”

    And then she was gone, taking her sandalwood scent and angelic brown eyes with her.

    Why did Agatha want her to come back? Why was their brief back and forth the most exciting thing that had happened to her all month? She hadn’t even gotten a name. Why on earth hadn’t she asked for her name? She huffed at her idiocy, fetching a well-needed cigarette from her jeans pocket with her lighter. She lit it, shielding the end from the wind that had picked up. The first inhale was always the best, would always feel like a weight being lifted off her shoulders. The second was always a disappointment, a reminder that she was destroying her lungs one drag at a time. Oh, well. She was going to die anyway.

    Her fingers started fiddling with a patch of grass, twisting and pulling to give her free hand something to do. This couldn’t be it, could it? Agatha couldn’t waste a way on the grounds of one of the world's most prestigious universities drunk and buzzed on nic for the foreseeable future. There had to be more.

    Scuffed Doc Martens filled her vision and Agatha looked up to see those pools of amber again.

    “I can’t leave you here, come on,” the brunette said, nodding for Agatha to get up.

    Brown eyes narrowed and Agatha lifted her cigarette to her lips to hide her smirk.

    “Because unlike you people, I’m a somewhat decent human being.” “You know,” Agatha began, breathing out the smoke in her mouth. “It’s awfully presumptuous of you to assume I’m one of those people.” The girl shrugged. “It doesn't take a genius to figure it out; it’s your accent. It screams old English money.”

    “Maybe I’m just a good actress,” Agatha answered.

    “It’s your attire,” the stranger went on. “Cartier watch, Tiffany earrings. That’s a Ralph Lauren button-up and I bet those jeans are Prada.” They were Prada.

    “Charity shops are gold mines these days,” Agatha replied nonchalantly. “You also smell like Tom Ford,” the brunette concluded.

    “And how would you know what Tom Ford smells like?” Agatha asked, quirking a brow.

    The girl’s lips lifted as if she were about to utter the grand reveal. “You’re also Agatha Harkness.”

    Agatha’s eyebrows lifted at that. “So if you know who I am, you rattling off that list of designer brands was…what? To boast about your knowledge?” “No,” the stranger said simply. “I just wanted to see how long you kept up the ruse.”

    Agatha took another drag before saying, “All night if I had to.” “Yeah, I figured.”

    “And what about you, stalker? You got a name?”

    Agatha hoped her tone masked how desperate she was to find out said name.

    “I do.”

    Agatha waited for her to disclose it.

    “But I won’t be telling you until you come with me,” she finished. Unfortunately, Agatha couldn’t grasp the irritation that flitted across her face. The brunette’s lips tugged into a smirk.

    “Which is…something you want.” She nodded for Agatha to get up again. “Come on then.”

    Agatha held her gaze, a challenge in her eyes but the brunette kept up, her own gaze unwavering. When Agatha realised they’d probably be here all night, she huffed, throwing her cigarette on the floor and stamping it out. “You can’t leave that there.”

    “Whoops,” Agatha said, getting up and dusting herself off. The girl had to be some kind of climate change activist. She picked up Agatha’s litter and disposed of it in the nearest rubbish bin. “Not a fan of littering?” Agatha taunted.

    The stranger ignored her. “Where are we going? What college are you in?” “I see you’re back to grilling me.”

    “I’m walking you home,” the girl dismissed.

    “How chivalrous,” Agatha grinned.

    “You’re drunk and it’s late.”

    “It’s one in the morning.”

    “It’s late.”

    Agatha tilted her head. “I thought you Spaniards loved partying till the sun came up.”

    “Now who’s being presumptuous?” the brunette scoffed.

    Agatha laughed, heading in the direction of her dorm room. “You’re telling me you’re not Spanish? I’ve spent enough summers in Marbella to know what a Spanish accent sounds like.”

    “Half.”

    “What?”

    “I’m half Spanish,” the girl replied. “I’m also Puerto Rican.” Agatha nodded.

    They fell into a somewhat comfortable silence and Agatha realised she’d walked right into a trap. She was going to have to spend the next eight minutes of her life with this complete and utter stranger who had somehow become the most intriguing person she’d ever met. Stealing glances at the beautiful brunette should’ve sounded the alarm bells in her head, should’ve told her to run for the fucking hills.

    She stayed put, walking in unison with this abomination of nature. “Would you like help carrying your books?” Agatha offered when it appeared the girl was struggling.

    “You ever carried books before?” she jeered.

    Agatha threw her hands up. “And this is why I don’t offer randoms help.” “Do you ever offer anyone help?”

    Agatha pulled a face. “You know, you’re awfully-” she cut herself off when the brunette dumped two thick books in her hands. “Judgy.” “Doesn’t everyone judge you?”

    Well, yes. You can’t come from English aristocracy and not be judged. Especially when you were a part of the Harkness dynasty “Not everyone’s brave enough to say it aloud.”

    “I’m not like everyone else,” the stranger shrugged.

    “Oh, no,” Agatha said, shaking her head patronisingly. “You’re not like other girls at all.”

    The girl sneered and Agatha smiled.

    Conversation, somehow, moved on. They spoke about Oxford, about how they had found the first month and it shocked Agatha when she realised they had a great deal in common. Oxford was too small. Word travelled too fast and you couldn’t go anywhere without recognising a face. They both yearned for freedom, to escape the shackles of false pretences and betrayals disguised behind wide smiles.

    “Then why’d you come to Oxford?” Agatha asked.

    Her companion shrugged. “It’s what I thought I wanted. I got a good scholarship, you know? Most of my expenses have been covered and after all this place is still Oxford.”

    Agatha couldn’t argue with that.

    “This is you,” the brunette said, stopping before the magnificent historic building that was Christ Church College. “Thanks for carrying my books.” Agatha off-loaded them onto the other girl. “It’s not like you gave me much choice when you dumped them on me.”

    “Hey, you offered,” the brunette smiled. She turned to leave. “Wait…what about you? I don’t want you walking around by yourself,” Agatha admitted.

    The brunette’s smile widened. “I’ll be alright, I’m in Merton. It’s two minutes away…barely.”

    Agatha nodded. “…Okay.”

    “And it’s Rio, by the way,” the girl called over her shoulder as she walked away.

    “Rio,” Agatha murmured to test the name out for herself. She liked it, liked the way it tasted in her mouth.

    Maybe going to Oxford hadn’t been a grave mistake, maybe it was something bigger, better, just like Agatha thought. Rio made it so.

    Azalea House, The Harkness Estate, Oxfordshire.

    July 2026.

    Three fucking years over and done with. How? How the bloody hell had time slipped through Agatha’s fingers like that? Her first month at Oxford felt like it would never end and now she was weeks away from graduating. It was insane to think about how much had changed. Those so-called friends she’d started her university journey with were no longer a factor. She’d made genuine connections, found real friendships in the most unlikely of places.

    She grinned as those friendships she spoke of drove down the drive. Inviting her mates to her house had been long overdue and with her mother on a work trip for the foreseeable future, it was now or never. Agatha put her hand up to shield her eyes from the sun and no sooner than she did, a parasol was lifted above her head. She turned to see her butler, Nigel, smiling from ear to ear.

    “There is absolutely no need to blind yourself.”

    “I am hardly blinding myself, Nigel,” Agatha replied.

    He tilted his head back and forth. “Then there is no need for you to get sunburnt.”

    Fair enough.

    “And before you ask, each guest room has been prepared and every surface has been dusted and polished and-”

    “I believe you,” Agatha interrupted before her butler began to ramble. “You always take such good care of our home.”

    Nigel beamed with pride. “Thank you, ma’am.”

    “Agatha,” Agatha corrected. She wondered how many times she was going to have to remind him. “I’m not my mother; there’s no need for formalities.”

    “Yes, but-”

    Natasha was up next, stepping into the space Jen had vacated. “Hello, darling.” She pressed a kiss to each of Agatha’s cheeks.

    Alice was without her signature orange stripes that brightened up her dark hair.

    “Likewise,” Jen muttered.

    Natasha and Agatha glanced at each other.

    Okay…so maybe inviting both of her best friends when they’d recently broken up wasn’t the smartest of ideas, but Agatha figured this week would sort them out. Come on, it had to, right? Their breakup had been stupid and forced proximity was the perfect cure for stupidity.

    Agatha’s home was, to put it lightly, the most extravagant limestone mansion Rio had ever laid eyes on. Google didn’t do it justice, and yes, Rio Googled it before she came. She’d been curious, sue her. The Grade I listed English country estate could fit Rio’s three-bedroom house in it four hundred times at least. On the way up the long drive, Rio spotted multiple ponds, a stable, and a tennis court. She knew at the back of the house there was also a maze, swimming pool, guest house and much more. Try as Rio might, she couldn’t pick her jaw off the floor as Agatha gave them a tour of her house. She rattled off facts about British royalty who had once lived in it, showed the girls her multiple living rooms, libraries, dining rooms and bedrooms and lastly, the kitchen. Every room was fitted with lavish furniture: polished oak, flamboyant rugs, sleek leather and velvet sofas and decadent chandeliers.

    “That pretty much sums it up,” Agatha concluded when she finished the tour.

    Rio shrugged. “It’s not all that.”

    Agatha laughed.

    Rio loved making Agatha laugh, it was one of her favourite things to do. “Sorry to disappoint,” she quipped.

    “You have such a lovely home, Agatha,” Scarlett told her. “Thank you,” Agatha replied. “I was thinking we head to the pool to start off with.”

    “Great plan,” Natasha said. “This heatwave is no joke. I’m sweating my tits off.”

    Everyone agreed.

    The heat in England was different. It sat thick and heavy on your shoulders and without a light breeze to help cool you down, the heat stuck to your body like clingfilm.

    “Brilliant, head to your rooms and meet around the pool in twenty,” Agatha said.

    Scarlett’s hand slipped into Rio’s and she tugged them into the corridor en route to their room. Conveniently, it was next to Agatha’s. Rio already knew she’d be in and out of there to steal her best friend’s clothes. They always shared clothes at university, but she’d have to commit to a two- minute walk which was now a two-second walk to do so.

Rio furrowed her eyebrows, walking over to them. The books consisted of an autobiography by biochemist Dorothy Crowfoot Hodgkin, a botanical encyclopaedia that covered all the plants on the estate and Carmilla by Sheridan Le Fanu. The novella had a note attached to it.

    This is the perfect opportunity for you to finally read it, you twat. Love, A x Rio scoffed, setting the book down.

    Rio silenced her with a kiss. Scarlett’s eyes narrowed when she drew back. “You’re cute, so I’m going to ignore that very obvious distraction.” “You think I’m cute?” Rio teased. “Wanna be my girlfriend?” Scarlett rolled her eyes, diverting her attention to their luggage cases. Rio didn’t know if it had been a good idea to bring her girlfriend. Agatha had invited her, but she figured it was probably because she felt obliged to. There was nothing to be done.

    “I can’t believe Agatha lives here,” Scarlett commented, swiping her dark, silky hair behind her ear to unzip her luggage.

    “Neither,” Rio agreed.

    “Feels weird that we’ll be the only couple though. I really thought Alice and Jen would’ve figured it out by now,” Scarlett went on. Alice and Jen’s break up had been unexpected. They’d both been tight- lipped on the details but from what Rio gathered it had been over nothing. Stupid, really. Why would you break up with the love of your life over something trivial?

    Rio nodded. “Me too, but they’ll figure it out.”

    “I think so too. The right people always end up together,” Scarlett smiled knowingly.

    Rio’s stomach twisted at the implication. It wasn’t that she didn’t love her girlfriend, she did. She just didn’t think she was in love with her. There was something missing. A spark or something. Love should help complete you, and with Scarlett there were still puzzle pieces out of place. If she were being honest, Rio still didn’t understand how she’d ended up with a girlfriend and they’d been dating for over two years. They were lab partners, in the same college and always ended up at the library at the same time. Naturally, a friendship blossomed and the next thing Rio knew Scarlett was asking her to be her girlfriend. Rio said yes because it stopped her from being with her.

    Rio couldn’t be with her.

    Oxford University, Oxfordshire.

    December 2023.

After drinking their body weight in cider at The Turf Tavern, Agatha declared that she needed some fresh air and invited Rio to join her. She lifted a cigarette to her lips, inhaled, exhaled and collapsed on the grass, a giggle floating out of her mouth.

Rio had never come across such beauty.

    Rio shook her head. “No, no, that’s not for me.”

    “You did it wrong,” Agatha cackled.

    I’d trust you with my life.

    “Not really, I’ve only known you a few months. You could be a murderer for all I know,” Rio joked.

    Rio’s lips curled.

    “Come on,” Agatha pouted. “For me?”

    Rio’s face scrunched. “You’re far too old to be doing that.” Agatha’s pout didn’t budge and the longer she held it, the more Rio felt she was neglecting a newborn puppy.

    “Fine,” she huffed.

    “Good,” Agatha grinned triumphantly.

    Rio tapped at the cigarette before lifting it to her lips once more. Agatha’s hand was back on her chest, her warmth, despite the chill in the air seeping through Rio’s skin.

    “In,” Agatha instructed.

    Rio inhaled.

    “Hold it,” Agatha continued, pressing her hand firmer against her chest. Why did time seem to stop? Why was Rio content with staring into dazzling grey eyes for the rest of her life?

    “Out,” Agatha finished.

    Rio blew the smoke out without dying this time.

    “Well, we’re friends, Aggs.” Rio didn’t look up, didn’t want to see whatever expression painted Agatha’s face.

    “Are we?” Came the swift reply.

    Probably not. They always felt like more.

    “Yeah,” Rio stuttered.

    “You don’t sound so sure. And just because we’re friends doesn't mean we can’t kiss,” Agatha added.

    Rio shook her head. “I can’t kiss you.”

    Because I’d want another, and another after that. I’d kiss you until I couldn’t breathe.

    “No.”

    “Good, gimme,” Agatha instructed.

    Agatha deadpanned. “I never said I didn’t like it, I said it was old school.” Rio tusked. “It’s not about what you said, it’s about how you said it.” Agatha rolled her eyes and Rio laughed.

Why did butterflies start fluttering in Rio’s stomach? Why did she feel lightheaded, and parched and out of breath? Why did it feel like her best friend was watching her hand clench when she wrote and her teeth nibbling at her bottom lip whilst she counted fucking oxygen bubbles? “You thirsty?” Agatha asked, breaking the long silence.

    Rio furrowed her eyebrows. Did she look as thirsty as she felt? “Just because I bought your favourite,” Agatha continued, fetching a chilled Lipton peach iced tea from her tiny designer bag. Rio honestly couldn’t believe that the bottle fit in the thing. “You know we’re not allowed drinks in here,” Rio replied instead of a ‘thank you’ or a ‘I could fucking kiss you for remembering and then bringing my favourite drink.’

    “Why do I-?”

    “Because you said you can’t drink in the lab, but does it count if I’m feeding you?”

    Rio swallowed the little saliva in her mouth. “…Okay.”

    She leaned in and Agatha caressed her jaw, tugging it open to lift the bottle to her lips. Grey eyes remained fixed on amber, kept looking at her like she was the most beautiful creature she’d ever laid eyes on.

    Rio wanted to devour her.

    Her throat bobbled as the ice-cold beverage slipped down her throat, grounding her to reality as she got lost in the motions of it all. Agatha pulled the bottle away and peach iced tea dribbled down Rio’s chin. Agatha was there to mop up the residue, swiping her thumb over the liquid and sucking it into her mouth. Rio wanted to kiss her so badly, almost moved to do so when she remembered she had a girlfriend. Why the fuck did she have a girlfriend?!

    “You’re welcome.”

At every bump in the road Agatha knew exactly what she needed. What had Rio done to deserve being understood so completely? “Baby?”

    Rio blinked and registered the sun on her skin and the repeated smack of a shuttlecock against rackets.

    “Hm?”

    “Let me guess, you were thinking about kinetic enzyme reaction rates?” Scarlett teased.

    Well…technically yes.

    “Oh, you know me so well.”

    “I’d like to think so,” Scarlett smiled. “I was saying about the…” “This won’t take all day,” Rio finished. “Maybe an hour or so. Can you handle that?” she quipped.

    Her girlfriend shrugged. “I could see how important this was to you. You want to win and I want to help.”

    Rio blinked. “I-”

What in the actual fuck was wrong with her? Who fucking has a nice, beautiful, attentive girlfriend and thinks about sleeping with their best friend?

    “I just…I…”

    Am the worst person on the planet.

Scarlett jumped into action, tapping the shuttle lightly over the net in the hopes of catching their opposition out. It almost worked but Agatha and her quick reflexes spoiled the tactical plan of action. Rio huffed. This was going to be a long game.

    “Wanda!” Agatha scolded.

    It was Wanda’s turn to pull a face. “Well, too bad!” she shouted. “Both teams need just two points to win!”

    Agatha rolled her eyes and turned her attention back to Rio. “Serve then.” Rio went to do so as Scarlett said: “We just need to win these last two rallies, we’ve got this!” she encouraged. Why did the encouragement feel like a bullet in the heart? Why was her girlfriend getting into this tournament just because Rio had been so passionate about it? Why was her girlfriend far too fucking good for her? What did Rio ever do to deserve her? Something sickly wedged itself in Rio’s gut and that urge to vomit that had been plaguing her all week was back. Guilt. Disgustingly well-placed guilt.

    Rio glared at her. “Seriously?”

    “Hey, I asked for the megaphone for a reason,” Wanda shrugged. Jen retrieved the shuttlecock and prepared to take her serve. “Don’t worry about it,” Scarlett reassured, noticing Rio’s tight grip around her racket. “The next point is ours.”

    Please stop being nice to me!

    Do you know how much I take you for granted?

    Do you know how many times I’ve wished you were someone else? Do you know that I’m probably going to ruin us?

    Jen served and Rio watched the shuttlecock float towards her. She didn’t move, she wasn’t even sure if she was breathing and it hit the ground. Someone, most likely Jen, screamed victoriously. The megaphone crackled, Wanda said something. There was movement, Natasha and Alice running off the stands. Laughter, Agatha’s. The world?

    The world fell away and Rio wanted to go with it.

    Dissociating was safe, not necessarily healthy, but always safe. Dissociating meant she didn’t have to live in the chaos she’d created for herself. Every emotion she’d ever felt kept vying for the surface and Rio wanted nothing to do with them. Guilt had struck her like a bolt of lightning. Sudden. Without permission. Without a care if she lived or died.

    She tilted her head to the blinding sun. The scorching heat burned her skin and blurred her vision. Maybe her throat was dry. Maybe she should sit down. Maybe she needed a glass of water. The badminton racket slipped from her sweaty hand.

    “Rio?” someone said at the edge of her mind.

    “Baby?”

    “Rio?!” another voice screamed.

    “Are you-”

    The next voice got cut off when Rio’s head hit the concrete and she blacked out.

    Chapter 8

The idiot played back-to-back games in the scorching heat without a single drop of water. Agatha watched it happen. Rio seemed to be in a daze, her olive skin had turned translucent before she keeled over. She screamed for Nigel and ran to her best friend’s aid. For a chilling moment, Agatha thought she might be dead. There was no rise and fall of her chest and her head had smashed into the concrete.     “Aggie?”

Until she was allowed into her new dorm room in August, Alice and her family agreed for Rio to stay with them. The initial plan was to alternate between her best friend’s house and Scarlett's, but obviously that wasn’t possible anymore. It dawned on Rio how many of her clothes were Agatha’s or had been bought by her. What’s yours is mine, Agatha’s voice said in her head. The words pushed a flurry of memories to the forefront, but one in particular stood out.

    Oxford University, Oxfordshire.

    September 2025.

    “There she is!” Agatha exclaimed.

    Rio spun on the spot, her grin wide as her best friend approached her. It had been somewhat of a tradition that they’d meet at the Radcliffe Camera at the start of every year. This is where they met after all and as much as Agatha would deny it, she was the sentimental type.

    “Here I am,” she replied, opening her arms.

    Agatha stepped into them, throwing her own around Rio’s neck. She took this as the perfect opportunity to pick Agatha up to twirl them. “Ri!” Agatha scolded through a laugh. “You’re going to make me drop it.” It was only then Rio realised her best friend was holding something. She set Agatha down and she shoved a white bag in her direction.

    Rio quirked a brow. “...Thank you?”

    Agatha pulled a face. “Can you not act all suspicious? I always get you stuff.”

    “Exactly. And I’ve told you to stop,” Rio told her.

    Agatha shrugged. “It’s a special occasion. It’s my last first day in this hell hole and your second to last.”

    “Hm,” Rio hummed, fishing out an envelope from her jeans pocket. “Good thing I got you this then.”

    Agatha squealed, snatching the envelope from her hold and replacing it with the white bag. They opened their gifts at the same time. Rio was greeted with three new outfits and Agatha-

    “Are you fucking kidding me?! Do you know how many books I can buy with this?!”

    “Ten…I think?” Rio teased.

    Agatha held up a hundred-pound gift card to Blackwell’s Book Shop. “Wait…” Agatha began, her excitement dying as quickly as it had spurred. “You don’t have a hundred to spare like that.”

    “For you I do,” Rio winked.

    “I’m being serious, Ri. I can’t accept this,” Agatha said, handing it back. “Yes, you can,” Rio reassured. “You know how much I worked over the summer. I set it aside just for that…I had to sacrifice buying something for my girlfriend but I’ll get her something next year,” she added with another wink.

    Agatha’s expression softened and she looked close to crying. “You know I can get my own books. Why’d you do this?”

    Rio cackled. “Because you’re my best friend and I love you? Plus every time you read your new books you’ll think of me and that’s worth a lot.” Agatha’s lips curled as she wiped at her eyes. “So this is psychological conditioning?”

    Rio shook her head playfully. “Only you would see it that way.” “And you,” Agatha countered. “We’re a match made in hell you and me.” “We’d destroy each other,” Rio mocked her own words before giggling. “You ready to finally finish your history degree?”

    “You have no idea how ready I am to graduate. I’d say the only thing I’m not ready for is not seeing your stupid face every day,” Agatha said. “Oh, really? That’s what I’m most excited about.”

    Agatha swatted her on the arm and Rio laughed.

    “Come on,” Agatha started, lacing her fingers with Rio’s even though she knew she shouldn’t. “We gotta go meet the girls at Ramen Korner before we’re late.”

    Rio let her lead the way and as she did she took in Agatha’s outfit from behind. She wore an almost golden off-the-shoulder knitted jumper lined with buttons at the hems, light-washed baggy jeans and a gold belt that shone in the autumn sunlight.

    “I like that jumper,” Rio commented. “Don’t be surprised when it ends up in my wardrobe.”

    “What’s mine is yours,” Agatha replied offhandedly.

    Two of the tops Agatha had given her that day stared back at Rio and she buried them beneath her pyjamas so she wouldn’t have to look at them. Speak of the devil.

    A grey eye appeared in the gap of the door that had creaked open. But of course Agatha wouldn’t bother knocking.

    “Can I come in?” she asked.

    “No,” Rio dismissed.

    “Rio,” Agatha tried.

    Rio’s answer and delivery were just the same. “No.”

    To nobody's surprise, Agatha came in anyway.

    “Are you fucking kidding me?”

    “Just-”

    “Get. Out,” Rio demanded, getting to her feet to push Agatha out of the room.

    “Just, wait, okay I-” Agatha dropped to her knees placing her hands on Rio’s.

    Rio’s eyes squeezed shut at the touch, at Agatha’s warmth seeping into her veins. When she felt Agatha’s forehead against her stomach, Rio wanted to sob. The intimacy of it all was disarming, made Rio forget that she was mad and that she hated her…almost.

    “Go away, Agatha,” Rio said although her voice had lost its venom. “Look,” Agatha began, lifting her head. “I know that- hey,” she soothed when Rio began to cry. “Don’t cry, Ri. I’ve got you.”

    “No you don’t,” Rio replied without missing a beat. “You don’t.” “But that’s why I’m here,” Agatha continued, grey eyes glazing over with their own tears. “I came to- I want to say that I’m- I’m…” she trailed off and Rio scoffed.

    Even now, even when it appeared she desperately wanted to, she couldn’t bring herself to apologise.

    “Leave me alone, Agatha.” It sounded like a plea because it was. Agatha’s eyebrows pinched, her nose scrunching as tears rolled down her cheeks. “I’ve ruined you,” she started as if she were going to go into a grand speech.

    “I’m just glad I did it first,” Rio finished, stopping it in its tracks. She didn’t want to hear it.

    Agatha huffed. “Baby-”

    “Don’t you dare call me that,” Rio bit out, her unshed tears finally falling. “If you care about me even a little bit, you’ll leave. You’ll actually listen to me for once in your fucking life.”

    Agatha sobbed and Rio wondered if this felt like that worship she’d described yesterday. She was on her knees pleading in whatever way that meant for her. Did this feel like devotion? Like humbling herself before a higher being.

    Without a word, Agatha got up and headed for the door.

    “I’m not coming down for breakfast,” Rio announced. “And when I leave later, if I never see you again, I’d be fine with that.”

    Agatha turned, a small smile lifting her lips regardless of the tears. “Liar.” Then she was gone.

    Even when Rio got into the waiting car an hour later, she never saw Agatha again.

    Oxford University, Oxfordshire.

    September 2026.

    Two months was a relatively short time for a post-argument break. On average they went without talking for three…four, if Agatha was especially pissed. It’s just what they did, screamed, shouted and insulted each other to the point of seemingly no return. Sometimes Rio would try to initiate some sort of communication until eventually giving up, and Agatha always re- entered Rio’s life as if nothing had happened.

    There was no staying away from Rio.

    Like clockwork, Agatha would randomly realise she was over whatever they’d been fighting about and want her best friend back. She usually brought gifts to help smooth things over and even though she had one with her this time, she decided she wouldn’t reveal it until they were good again. The last thing she wanted was for Rio to think she was manipulating her. And sure, yeah, Agatha did do that, but she wouldn’t today. Walking the streets of the town centre during the first weeks of the semester without actually attending university was strange. Agatha hadn’t anticipated she’d feel this way. She’d been so ready to finish her degree that it never occurred to her that she’d miss university life. The buzz that came with seeing her friends again, decorating a new room, starting a new module, all things she wouldn’t be doing for the first time in three years. She shoved those thoughts aside to continue her pursuit. Life 360 told Agatha Rio was at the Radcliffe Camera, which, course. Where else would she be? Agatha was just happy her best friend hadn’t stopped sharing her location with her. Anxiety began to creep in and Agatha fiddled with the keys of her new black Range Rover, courtesy of her mother, to give her fingers something to do. The car was a long overdue apology for the hell her parents put her through this summer. Technically, Agatha couldn’t be blamed for how she tried to resolve conflicts; it wasn’t like she had the best role models.

    Stop it.

    Right, because she was supposed to be taking accountability now and all that vile, disgusting shit. She huffed when she tried to turn onto Radcliffe Square and got stuck behind a cluster of tourists. They took up the narrow pathway turning Agatha’s speed walk into a crawl. There was nothing more aggravating than snail-pace walkers, especially when the dome structure of the Radcliffe Camera was just out of reach.

    Enough was enough. Agatha barged her way through, ignoring the grunts and mumbles of protest. There was no sign of Rio by the entrance so Agatha began to make her way around. She prayed to Whoever that her best friend was outside and not in. There would be no getting past security without a valid student ID.

    Hope was dying as quickly as it had sparked because Agatha was almost halfway around the building and there was still no sign of Rio. Was she really inside? Had Life 360 lied to her and she wasn’t here at all? Had the universe decided today the stars would not align? Well, the universe could go fuck itself. How dare it get in the way of-

    Oh…there she is. Apologies universe.

    Her best friend sat cross-legged in the grass clicking away at her green iPod shuffle, presumably looking for a song considering her earphones were in, surrounded by books she must’ve acquired from the library. A plum off-the- shoulder jumper complemented her olive skin and baggy dark-washed jeans clung to her legs. Agatha watched her run a hand through her raven hair and more of her face was exposed to the autumn sunlight. Had Rio bought a new blush? Her cheekbones were painted in some shade of burgundy. She looked beautiful, she was beautiful.

    Agatha could watch her forever.

    But watching wouldn’t resolve things so she looked left and right to make sure the coast was clear before hopping over the black fence, narrowly missing being stabbed by one of the spikes. Who told her to wear jeans? Parkour was not an activity to be doing in denim. Two girls sitting in one of the nooks in the wall raised an eyebrow at her and Agatha waved them off, impolitely gesturing to them to mind their own business.

    In a few steps she was in Rio’s orbit and with a wavering voice, she asked: “This seat taken?”

    Rio froze, Agatha’s voice ringing in her ears and paralysing her. Her gaze was unmoving, fixed on her iPod as if she were hoping she’d made the voice up. Agatha’s cheeks began to burn, up there with regret, love and cowardice, embarrassment was in the top four emotions she hated feeling.

    Grave fucking mistakes.

    She shouldn’t have come.

    Without warning, amber eyes snapped to grey and Agatha almost lept out of her skin.

    “Jesus.”

    “What are you doing here?”

    Agatha didn’t have a clue anymore.

    “I- I just…” She sucked in a breath to collect herself. “I came to apologise,” Agatha announced.

    Rio quirked a brow. “You came to apologise?”

    Agatha pulled a face. “You didn’t need to say it like that.” “Like what?”

    “Like you weren’t expecting it,” Agatha replied, taking a seat beside her best friend regardless of the lack of invitation.

    It was almost comical how much this mirrored their first interaction all those years ago.

    Rio rolled her eyes. “Because I wasn’t. You notoriously don’t apologise.” “I-” Agatha began but cut herself off because there was no point in lying. “New year, new me.”

    “We’ve been in the year of 2026 for nine months now,” Rio stated. “Just,” Agatha held out a hand for silence. She sucked in another breath to get out, “I’m sorry, Ri. Truly, sincerely, I’m sorry.”

    Rio’s head jerked an inch, seemingly startled that Agatha had actually said it. “You…” She cleared her throat. “And what exactly are you sorry for?” Agatha bit her cheek to stop herself from snapping at her. Of course the little shit was going to make her squirm.

    “I’m sorry for running. I’m sorry for disregarding your boundaries. I’m sorry for all the time I manipulated you. I’m sorry for causing that fight during that thunderstorm in second year. I’m sorry for taking off the blindfold last time we fucked. I’m sorry for stealing your Greggs sausage roll in first year. I’m sorry for everything, all of it. I am so sorry, Ri,” Agatha finished.

    She was surprisingly teary. Agatha dabbed at her eyes with the sleeves of her pale green button-up. Clearly Rio was in the mood to torture her because she’d failed to say a word. The weight of Agatha’s words hung in the air and she questioned her entire existence.

    Fuck me.

    This is why she didn’t apologise.

    “Thank you,” Rio finally said. “I really appreciate it.”

    Agatha’s lips curled, the action forcing her unshed tears to fall. Okay…so apologising wasn’t so bad after all.

    “And I’m sorry that I made it seem like being with you would be the end of the world,” Rio admitted.

    Agatha’s heartbeat stuttered. She wasn’t expecting that. “I-” “I made mistakes,” Rio interrupted. “I hurt a lot of people and it all could’ve been avoided if I were willing to face the truth.” Agatha nodded slowly. “But you were right to be apprehensive. The second we could be together I did a complete 180, you should always trust your gut,” she said, echoing her words from their fight two months ago. It was Rio’s turn to nod. Amber eyes remained fixed on grey, a sad knowing in her gaze.

    “Do you think we could’ve fallen in love without hurting each other?” Agatha whispered, the words leaving her mouth before she could process them.

    Rio’s eyes dropped to her lap. “I- I don’t know. That’s the only way I know how to love someone.”

    Agatha’s heart ached. She understood her best friend so much, felt the confession deep in her soul.

    “Me too. But I…I want to be better at it…for you,” Agatha began, only continued when Rio was looking at her again. “I can’t promise you that I’ll be magically healed and all my avoidant tendencies will vanish into thin air. We’ll probably still argue and say shit we shouldn’t but I’ll try my best, I’ll try my hardest so that we can make this work.”

    “You’d do that for me?” Rio whispered.

    Agatha took her hands and squeezed. “I’d literally do anything for you.” “Why?”

    “Because I love you more than I could ever articulate.”

    Rio’s eyebrows pinched and she let out a sigh that appeared to lift the weight off her shoulders. “I love you so fucking much, Agatha. I want to do life with you in whatever that looks like.”

    There was no stopping the smile that flew onto her lips. “So does this mean I’m your girlfriend now-?” she got cut off when Rio crashed their lips together.

    Agatha’s entire body relaxed as she melted into Rio’s arms, that comforting fragrance of sandalwood filling her nose. Her heartbeat thudded wildly in her ear and it was like the organ had been revived, like it hadn’t been working at its full capacity until now. Maybe it couldn’t if she wasn’t in Rio’s orbit.

    “Depends what my present is,” Rio teased, pulling back.

    Agatha’s jaw dropped. “How did you know I got you something?” “Because I know you,” Rio giggled.

    I know you.

    A blessing and a curse, but maybe it was just a blessing. Agatha huffed before fishing out a red box from her jeans pocket to give to her. When Rio opened it, her lips curled into a smirk.

    “Presumptuous.”

    A silver ‘A’ necklace glistened in the sunlight.

    “Inevitable,” Agatha corrected. “I was hoping you would give me yours,” she went on, nodding to the ‘R’ pendant that hung off Rio’s neck. She raised an eyebrow. “You don’t wear silver,” Rio said, indicating to her assortment of gold necklaces.

    Agatha shrugged. “I’m willing to make an exception.”

    Rio shook her head playfully as she removed the necklace she hadn’t taken off since the day Agatha gave it to her. She encouraged Agatha to turn to put the necklace on its new owner. Once she was finished, Agatha encouraged her girlfriend to do the same.

    Girlfriend.

    How it just made sense.

    Rio was facing her again, a smile on her lips.

    “I think this is the dykiest thing we’ve ever done,” Agatha joked. Rio laughed, Agatha’s favourite sound, and the cuteness aggression took over. She moved to smother her girlfriend in kisses.

    “That tickles!” Rio giggled, repeatedly swatting her on the arm. Agatha eventually relented, cupping Rio’s face to draw her in to press their foreheads together. Rio sighed, lifting a hand to thread through Agatha’s hair.

    “Let’s try not to destroy each other,” Rio murmured.

    “Where’s the fun in that?” Agatha winked.
